export function serializeFictionalRecord(value){requireFact(arguments.length===1,'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');return serialize(value,profiles.RECORD,true,'RESOURCE_RECORD_BYTES');}
export const permittedPublicLiterals=Object.freeze(['fictional'+String.fromCharCode(64)+'example.invalid']);
const patterns=[['ANTHROPIC_KEY',/sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/g],['API_KEY',/sk-(?:proj-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{40,}/g],['JWT',/eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,}/g],['PRIVATE_KEY_HEADER',/-----BEGIN (?:RSA |EC |OPENSSH )?PRIVATE KEY-----/g],['EMAIL',/[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}/g],['AUTH_URL_CODE',/[?&](?:access_token|refresh_token|code)=[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,}/gi]];
function* emailMatches(text){const boundary=/(?<![A-Za-z0-9._%+-])[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}/g,sticky=/[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}/y;let cursor=0;
  while(cursor<text.length){let m=null;if(cursor>0&&/[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]/.test(text[cursor-1])){sticky.lastIndex=cursor;m=sticky.exec(text);}if(!m){boundary.lastIndex=cursor;m=boundary.exec(text);}if(!m)break;yield{index:m.index,length:m[0].length};cursor=m.index+m[0].length;}}
const jwtChar=n=>(n>=65&&n<=90)||(n>=97&&n<=122)||(n>=48&&n<=57)||n===95||n===45;
function* jwtMatches(text){let first=null,second=null,at=0,afterMatch=0;while(at<=text.length){const start=at;while(at<text.length&&jwtChar(text.charCodeAt(at)))at++;const third={start,end:at};
  if(first&&second&&second.end-second.start>=12&&third.end-third.start>=12){const from=Math.max(first.start,afterMatch);if(first.end-from>=15){const found=text.slice(from,first.end-12).indexOf('eyJ');if(found>=0){const index=from+found;yield{index,length:third.end-index};afterMatch=third.end;}}}
  first=second;second=third;if(at===text.length)break;if(text.charCodeAt(at)===46){at++;continue;}first=null;second=null;at++;}}
function* regexMatches(re,text){re.lastIndex=0;for(const m of text.matchAll(re))yield{index:m.index,length:m[0].length};}
export function createBudget(){return{hits:0,metadataByteCharge:0};}
export function sensitiveSpans(bytes,budget,escapeAware){
  requireFact(Buffer.isBuffer(bytes)&&budget&&typeof escapeAware==='boolean');const text=bytes.toString('latin1'),found=[];
  for(const [patternId,re] of patterns)for(const m of patternId==='EMAIL'?emailMatches(text):patternId==='JWT'?jwtMatches(text):regexMatches(re,text)){
    let start=m.index;if(escapeAware&&start>0&&text[start-1]==='\\'){let n=0;for(let i=start-1;i>=0&&text[i]==='\\';i--)n++;if(n%2===1){if(/^u[0-9A-Fa-f]{4}/.test(text.slice(start,start+5)))start+=5;else if('ntrbf'.includes(text[start]))start++;}}
    const end=m.index+m.length;if(start>=end)continue;if(patternId==='EMAIL'&&permittedPublicLiterals.includes(text.slice(start,end)))continue;
    requireFact(budget.hits<resourcePolicy.maxPatternHits,'RESOURCE_PATTERN_HITS');budget.hits++;found.push({startByte:start,endByte:end,patternIds:[patternId]});}
  found.sort((a,b)=>a.startByte-b.startByte||a.endByte-b.endByte);const merged=[];
  for(const s of found){const last=merged.at(-1);if(last&&s.startByte<last.endByte){last.endByte=Math.max(last.endByte,s.endByte);last.patternIds=[...new Set([...last.patternIds,...s.patternIds])];}else merged.push({...s});}return merged;
}
export function chargeRow(budget,row){const encoded=JSON.stringify(row,null,2);requireFact(/^[\x20-\x7e\n]*$/.test(encoded)&&!encoded.includes('\\'),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');
  const charge=Buffer.byteLength(encoded)+encoded.split('\n').length*8+2;requireFact(budget.metadataByteCharge+charge<=resourcePolicy.maxMetadataByteCharge,'RESOURCE_METADATA_BYTES');budget.metadataByteCharge+=charge;}
export function childPath(parent,suffix){requireFact(Buffer.byteLength(parent)+Buffer.byteLength(suffix)<=resourcePolicy.maxEntryPathBytes,'RESOURCE_ENTRY_PATH');return parent+suffix;}
export const workerRefusalCodes=Object.freeze(['RAW_STDOUT_DUPLICATE','RESOURCE_PATTERN_HITS','RESOURCE_METADATA_BYTES','RESOURCE_ENTRY_PATH','RESOURCE_PUBLIC_BYTES','RESOURCE_COPY_BYTES','RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT','PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED','TRANSFORM_STARTUP']);
export const procedureCodes=Object.freeze([...workerRefusalCodes,'RESOURCE_RECORD_BYTES','TRANSFORM_TIMEOUT','TRANSFORM_OOM','TRANSFORM_EXIT','TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL','TRANSFORM_EXIT_UNCERTAIN','JOURNAL_PERSIST_UNCERTAIN','DISPATCH_BINDING_INVALID','PUBLIC_COPY_INVALID','PUBLIC_COPY_PIN_INVALID']);
export function deriveTransformCode(e){
  if(e.observationState==='UNKNOWN_AFTER_CALLBACK_FAILURE'||(e.workerCreated&&!e.exitObserved))return'TRANSFORM_EXIT_UNCERTAIN';
  if(e.deadlineExpired)return'TRANSFORM_TIMEOUT';if(e.parentErrorCode==='ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY')return'TRANSFORM_OOM';
  if(e.parentErrorCode==='PROTOCOL_REFUSED'||e.parentErrorCode==='WORKER_ERROR')return'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL';
  if(e.exitObserved&&e.exitCode!==0)return'TRANSFORM_EXIT';if(e.workerOutcomeCode!==null)return e.workerOutcomeCode;
  if(!e.workerCreated)return'TRANSFORM_STARTUP';
  if(e.workerConstructionAttempted&&e.workerOnlineObserved&&e.workerSourceVerified&&e.resultMessageObserved&&e.exitObserved&&e.exitCode===0&&!e.terminationRequested)return null;
  return'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL';
}
export function unknownTransformEvidence(workerPin){return{observationState:'UNKNOWN_AFTER_CALLBACK_FAILURE',workerPin,policyId:resourcePolicy.policyId,errorCode:'TRANSFORM_EXIT_UNCERTAIN',forceRequesterExit:true,deadlineMs:resourcePolicy.workerDeadlineMs,terminationGraceMs:resourcePolicy.terminationGraceMs};}
export const responseCopyPolicy='Finite ASCII screening; unknown/escaped/unicode forms and privacy absence unproved. With JSON redaction, parse/serialize can drop duplicate keys, reorder integer keys and normalize or lose numeric representation/precision. Indexed span offsets disclose lengths; marker metadata names actual replacements, but literal marker text can look identical. No-hit complete JSON retains exact bytes. Lone surrogates within a redacted string can become U+FFFD; unrelated strings retain their parsed representation. Marker counters may have harmless gaps after discarded traversal or key collisions. Byte fallback screening is weaker for embedded escaped tokens and cannot establish usable review acceptance. No complete/verbatim claim after transformation, withholding or capture/storage failure; no private raw store or original/span hashes after redaction.';
export function strictEvidence(status,parserPin){return{stage:'RAW_STDOUT_BEFORE_REDACTION',status,parserPin:{...parserPin},code:status==='DUPLICATE_REFUSED'?'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA':null};}
export function validateStrictEvidence(e,parserPin){exactObject(e,['stage','status','parserPin','code']);requireFact(e.stage==='RAW_STDOUT_BEFORE_REDACTION');
  requireFact(['PASS','DUPLICATE_REFUSED','MALFORMED_OR_INCOMPLETE','NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD'].includes(e.status));exactObject(e.parserPin,['path','sha256','bytes']);requireFact(JSON.stringify(e.parserPin)===JSON.stringify(parserPin));requireFact(e.code===(e.status==='DUPLICATE_REFUSED'?'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA':null));return e;}
const streamKeys=['base64','originalSha256','publicSha256','originalRetainedBytes','publicRetainedBytes','observedBytes','truncated','redactedSpans','mode','coordinate','incompleteReasons','normalizationApplied','originalHashWithheldReason'];
const modes=['JSON_ENVELOPE','BYTE_FALLBACK','BYTE_FALLBACK_AFTER_JSON_ERROR','TRUNCATED_PREFIX_WITHHELD','INCOMPLETE_STREAM_WITHHELD'];
export function validatePublicResponseArtifact(bytes,parserPin){
  let a;try{a=parsePublicCopyStrictJson(bytes);}catch(e){throw new Refusal(e.code==='RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT'?e.code:'PUBLIC_COPY_INVALID');}
  exactObject(a,['scope','stdout','stderr','exitCode','closeObserved','screenLimit','streamCompletenessLimit','receiverBytesProved','automaticRetry','resourcePolicy','strictParseEvidence']);
  exactObject(a.resourcePolicy,Object.keys(resourcePolicy));requireFact(JSON.stringify(a.resourcePolicy)===JSON.stringify(resourcePolicy));validateStrictEvidence(a.strictParseEvidence,parserPin);requireFact(a.strictParseEvidence.status!=='DUPLICATE_REFUSED');
  requireFact(serializePublicCopy(a).equals(bytes));assertPublicCopyScreen(bytes);
  requireFact(a.scope==='PUBLIC RESPONSE COPY; decoded JSON leaves or labelled byte fallback; incomplete streams withheld'&&a.screenLimit===responseCopyPolicy&&a.receiverBytesProved===false&&a.automaticRetry===false);
  requireFact(a.streamCompletenessLimit==='Timeout/child error/missing close/overflow/signal imply withheld streams; stdin error or kill count alone does not, so a closed early-EOF result remains inspectable. Not a receiver or process-tree proof.');
  requireFact(typeof a.closeObserved==='boolean'&&(a.exitCode===null||Number.isSafeInteger(a.exitCode)));
  const decoded={},budget=createBudget();let charge=0,rows=0;
  const screen=b=>screenSensitive(b,budget,true);
  const jsonScreen=v=>{if(typeof v==='string')screenSensitive(Buffer.from(v),budget,false);
    else if(Array.isArray(v))v.forEach(jsonScreen);else if(v&&typeof v==='object')for(const key of Object.keys(v)){screenSensitive(Buffer.from(key),budget,false);jsonScreen(v[key]);}};
  for(const name of ['stdout','stderr']){const s=a[name],withheld=s?.mode==='TRUNCATED_PREFIX_WITHHELD'||s?.mode==='INCOMPLETE_STREAM_WITHHELD';
    exactObject(s,withheld?streamKeys:[...streamKeys,'jsonTransformError']);requireFact(modes.includes(s.mode));requireFact(name==='stdout'||s.mode!=='JSON_ENVELOPE');
    requireFact(typeof s.base64==='string'&&s.base64.length<=Math.ceil((name==='stdout'?resourcePolicy.publicStdoutBytes:resourcePolicy.publicStderrBytes)/3)*4);
    const b=Buffer.from(s.base64,'base64');requireFact(b.toString('base64')===s.base64);requireFact(b.length<=(name==='stdout'?resourcePolicy.publicStdoutBytes:resourcePolicy.publicStderrBytes));
    requireFact(Number.isSafeInteger(s.originalRetainedBytes)&&s.originalRetainedBytes>=0&&s.originalRetainedBytes<=(name==='stdout'?resourcePolicy.originalStdoutBytes:resourcePolicy.originalStderrBytes));
    requireFact(s.publicRetainedBytes===b.length&&s.publicSha256===hash(b));requireFact(s.originalSha256===null||/^[A-F0-9]{64}$/.test(s.originalSha256));
    requireFact(Number.isSafeInteger(s.observedBytes)&&s.observedBytes>=s.originalRetainedBytes&&typeof s.truncated==='boolean'&&Array.isArray(s.redactedSpans)&&Array.isArray(s.incompleteReasons)&&typeof s.normalizationApplied==='boolean');
    if(withheld){requireFact(b.length===0&&s.originalSha256===null&&s.redactedSpans.length===0&&s.coordinate===null&&!s.normalizationApplied&&s.incompleteReasons.length>0);
      requireFact(s.mode==='TRUNCATED_PREFIX_WITHHELD'?s.truncated:!s.truncated);requireFact(s.originalHashWithheldReason==='Incomplete stream may contain a partial secret below finite pattern thresholds');}
    else{requireFact(!s.truncated&&s.incompleteReasons.length===0&&s.coordinate===(s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'?'DECODED_STRING_UTF8_BYTE_OFFSETS':'ORIGINAL_RETAINED_BYTE_OFFSETS'));
      requireFact(s.jsonTransformError===(s.mode==='BYTE_FALLBACK_AFTER_JSON_ERROR'?'JSON_TRANSFORM_ERROR':null));
      requireFact(s.normalizationApplied===(s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'&&s.redactedSpans.length>0));
      requireFact(s.originalHashWithheldReason===(s.redactedSpans.length?'Original/span hash could confirm a low-entropy redacted value':null));
      requireFact(s.redactedSpans.length?s.originalSha256===null:s.originalSha256===hash(b));}
    for(const row of s.redactedSpans){exactObject(row,s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'?['startByte','endByte','patternIds','marker','entryPath','kind','coordinate']:['startByte','endByte','patternIds','marker']);
      requireFact(Number.isSafeInteger(row.startByte)&&Number.isSafeInteger(row.endByte)&&row.startByte>=0&&row.endByte>row.startByte&&Array.isArray(row.patternIds)&&row.patternIds.length>0&&row.patternIds.every(x=>patterns.some(([id])=>id===x)));
      requireFact(/^<REDACTED(?:_KEY)?:[0-9]+>$/.test(row.marker));if(s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'){requireFact(typeof row.entryPath==='string'&&/^\$(?:(?:\[array:[0-9]+\]|\[object-entry:[0-9]+\])(?:\.value)?)*$/.test(row.entryPath)&&Buffer.byteLength(row.entryPath)<=resourcePolicy.maxEntryPathBytes);requireFact(['VALUE','KEY'].includes(row.kind)&&row.coordinate==='DECODED_STRING_UTF8_BYTE_OFFSETS');}
      chargeRow({get metadataByteCharge(){return charge;},set metadataByteCharge(n){charge=n;}},row);rows++;requireFact(rows<=resourcePolicy.maxPatternHits);}
    if(name==='stdout'){requireFact((a.strictParseEvidence.status==='PASS')===(s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'));requireFact((a.strictParseEvidence.status==='MALFORMED_OR_INCOMPLETE')===(s.mode==='BYTE_FALLBACK'));requireFact((a.strictParseEvidence.status==='NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD')===withheld);requireFact(s.mode!=='BYTE_FALLBACK_AFTER_JSON_ERROR');if(s.mode==='BYTE_FALLBACK'){let validJson=false;try{parsePublicStdoutStrictJson(b);validJson=true;}catch(e){if(e.code==='RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT')throw e;requireFact(['JSON_SYNTAX','JSON_DUPLICATE'].includes(e.code));}requireFact(!validJson);}}
    if(s.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'){requireFact(a.strictParseEvidence.status==='PASS');let v;try{v=parsePublicStdoutStrictJson(b);}catch(e){throw new Refusal(e.code==='RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT'?e.code:'PUBLIC_COPY_INVALID');}jsonScreen(v);}else screen(b);
    decoded[name]=b;
  }
  if(a.strictParseEvidence.status==='NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD')requireFact(a.stdout.mode.endsWith('_WITHHELD'));
  return{artifact:a,decoded,publicResponseHashes:{stdout:hash(decoded.stdout),stderr:hash(decoded.stderr)}};
}
const bools=['closed','closeObserved','exitObserved','spawnAttempted','spawnObserved','spawnFailure','timedOut','overflow','stdinFinished','writableFinished','senderComplete','stdoutTruncated','stderrTruncated'];
const integers=['exitCode','stdoutObservedBytes','stderrObservedBytes','stdinBytesQueuedOrWritten','killAttemptCount','killAttemptsOmitted','wallMs'];
const errors=['childError','killError','sendException','stdinError'];
const safeCodes=['EPIPE','ENOENT','EACCES','EPERM','ENOSPC','EIO','EBADF','EINVAL','ERR_ASSERTION','ERR_STREAM_DESTROYED','ERR_STREAM_WRITE_AFTER_END','UNSPECIFIED_ERROR','KILL_RETURNED_FALSE','EOF','ECONNRESET','UNRECOGNIZED_CODE'];
const factKeys=[...bools,...integers,...errors,'signal','settlementReason'];
const projectionLimit='Observed state at projection time; no content/environment/error messages. Unknown codes/reasons are labelled, not copied. Does not prove receiver bytes or process-tree closure.';
export function derivedSenderCompletion(e){return e.closeObserved&&e.spawnObserved&&!e.spawnFailure&&e.childError===null&&!e.timedOut&&!e.overflow&&e.stdinError===null&&e.sendException===null&&e.stdinFinished&&e.writableFinished;}
export function captureSenderOutput(output){
  const required=[...factKeys,'stdoutBuffer','stderrBuffer'],optional=['stdout','stderr','killAttempts','receiverBytesProved'];const keys=Object.keys(output);requireFact(required.every(k=>keys.includes(k))&&keys.every(k=>required.includes(k)||optional.includes(k)),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');exactObject(output,keys,'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');const out={};
  for(const k of bools){requireFact(typeof output[k]==='boolean','UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');out[k]=output[k];}
  for(const k of integers){const v=output[k];requireFact(v===null||Number.isSafeInteger(v)&&(k==='exitCode'||v>=0),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');out[k]=v;}
  for(const k of errors){const v=output[k];if(v===null){out[k]=null;continue;}if(typeof v==='object')exactObject(v,['code'],'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');const code=typeof v==='string'?v:v.code;requireFact(typeof code==='string','UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');out[k]=Object.freeze({code:safeCodes.includes(code)?code:'UNRECOGNIZED_CODE'});}
  requireFact(output.signal===null||typeof output.signal==='string','UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');out.signal=output.signal===null?null:/^SIG[A-Z0-9]{1,12}$/.test(output.signal)?output.signal:'UNRECOGNIZED_SIGNAL';
  requireFact(typeof output.settlementReason==='string','UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');out.settlementReason=['SPAWN_THROW','CLOSE','EXIT_WITHOUT_CLOSE_GRACE_EXPIRED','TIMEOUT_GRACE_EXPIRED','OVERFLOW_GRACE_EXPIRED','STDIN_ERROR_GRACE_EXPIRED','CHILD_ERROR_GRACE_EXPIRED','SEND_EXCEPTION_GRACE_EXPIRED'].includes(output.settlementReason)?output.settlementReason:'UNRECOGNIZED_SETTLEMENT';
  for(const [key,limit] of [['stdoutBuffer',resourcePolicy.originalStdoutBytes],['stderrBuffer',resourcePolicy.originalStderrBytes]]){requireFact(Buffer.isBuffer(output[key])&&output[key].length<=limit,'RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT');out[key]=Buffer.from(output[key]);}
  requireFact(out.closed===out.closeObserved&&out.senderComplete===derivedSenderCompletion(out),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');return Object.freeze(out);
}
export function projectSender(capture,stage,stdout=capture.stdoutBuffer,stderr=capture.stderrBuffer){requireFact(['BEFORE_PUBLIC_RESPONSE_MUTATION','AFTER_PUBLIC_RESPONSE_MUTATION'].includes(stage),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');return Object.freeze({...Object.fromEntries(factKeys.map(k=>[k,capture[k]])),stdoutRetainedAtProjectionBytes:stdout.length,stderrRetainedAtProjectionBytes:stderr.length,projectionStage:stage,projectionLimit});}
export function validateWorkerRequest(dto,workerPin){exactObject(dto,['kind','workerPin','resourcePolicyId','senderBefore','stdout','stderr'],'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');
  requireFact(dto.kind==='RESPONSE_TRANSFORM_REQUEST'&&dto.resourcePolicyId===resourcePolicy.policyId&&JSON.stringify(dto.workerPin)===JSON.stringify(workerPin),'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');
  exactObject(dto.senderBefore,[...factKeys,'stdoutRetainedAtProjectionBytes','stderrRetainedAtProjectionBytes','projectionStage','projectionLimit'],'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');
  requireFact(dto.stdout instanceof ArrayBuffer&&dto.stderr instanceof ArrayBuffer,'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');
  const c=captureSenderOutput({...Object.fromEntries(factKeys.map(k=>[k,dto.senderBefore[k]])),stdoutBuffer:Buffer.from(dto.stdout),stderrBuffer:Buffer.from(dto.stderr)});
  requireFact(JSON.stringify(projectSender(c,'BEFORE_PUBLIC_RESPONSE_MUTATION'))===JSON.stringify(dto.senderBefore),'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');return c;
}
export function validateWorkerOutcome(m,parserPin){
  if(m?.type==='REFUSAL'){exactObject(m,['type','errorCode','strictParseEvidence','publicResponseHashes'],'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');requireFact(workerRefusalCodes.includes(m.errorCode)&&m.publicResponseHashes===null,'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');if(m.strictParseEvidence!==null)validateStrictEvidence(m.strictParseEvidence,parserPin);
    requireFact((m.errorCode==='RAW_STDOUT_DUPLICATE')===(m.strictParseEvidence?.status==='DUPLICATE_REFUSED'),'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');return m;}
  exactObject(m,['type','copy','sha256','bytes','strictParseEvidence'],'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');requireFact(m.type==='SUCCESS'&&m.copy instanceof ArrayBuffer&&m.bytes===m.copy.byteLength&&m.bytes<=resourcePolicy.maxCanonicalCopyBytes&&m.sha256===hash(Buffer.from(m.copy)),'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');validateStrictEvidence(m.strictParseEvidence,parserPin);requireFact(m.strictParseEvidence.status!=='DUPLICATE_REFUSED','TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');return m;
}
export function validateTransformEvidence(e,workerPin){
  if(e?.observationState==='UNKNOWN_AFTER_CALLBACK_FAILURE'){exactObject(e,Object.keys(unknownTransformEvidence(workerPin)),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');requireFact(JSON.stringify(e)===JSON.stringify(unknownTransformEvidence(workerPin)),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');return e;}
  exactObject(e,['workerPin','policyId','workerConstructionAttempted','workerCreated','workerOnlineObserved','workerSourceVerified','resultMessageObserved','exitObserved','deadlineExpired','terminationRequested','exitCode','errorCode','parentErrorCode','workerOutcomeCode','observedElapsedMs','deadlineMs','terminationGraceMs'],'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');
  requireFact(JSON.stringify(e.workerPin)===JSON.stringify(workerPin)&&e.policyId===resourcePolicy.policyId,'IDENTITY_MISMATCH');requireFact(e.deadlineMs===resourcePolicy.workerDeadlineMs&&e.terminationGraceMs===resourcePolicy.terminationGraceMs,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
  for(const k of ['workerConstructionAttempted','workerCreated','workerOnlineObserved','workerSourceVerified','resultMessageObserved','exitObserved','deadlineExpired','terminationRequested'])requireFact(typeof e[k]==='boolean','UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');
  requireFact(Number.isSafeInteger(e.observedElapsedMs)&&e.observedElapsedMs>=0&&(e.exitCode===null||Number.isSafeInteger(e.exitCode)),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');
  requireFact([null,'ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY','PROTOCOL_REFUSED','WORKER_ERROR'].includes(e.parentErrorCode)&&[null,...workerRefusalCodes].includes(e.workerOutcomeCode),'UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA');
  requireFact(e.errorCode===deriveTransformCode(e),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');requireFact(e.errorCode!=='TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL'||['PROTOCOL_REFUSED','WORKER_ERROR'].includes(e.parentErrorCode),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
  if(!e.workerCreated)requireFact(!e.workerOnlineObserved&&!e.workerSourceVerified&&!e.resultMessageObserved&&!e.exitObserved&&e.exitCode===null&&!e.terminationRequested&&e.parentErrorCode===null&&e.workerOutcomeCode===null,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
  if(e.workerCreated)requireFact(e.workerConstructionAttempted,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');if(e.exitObserved)requireFact(e.workerCreated&&e.exitCode!==null,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');else requireFact(e.exitCode===null,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
  if(e.workerSourceVerified)requireFact(e.workerCreated,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');if(e.resultMessageObserved&&e.workerOutcomeCode!=='TRANSFORM_STARTUP')requireFact(e.workerSourceVerified,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
  if(e.workerCreated&&!e.exitObserved)requireFact(e.terminationRequested,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');return e;
}
export function classifyResponse(output,parsed){
  if(output.senderComplete!==true)return'SENDER_INCOMPLETE';
  if(output.exitCode===0&&output.closed&&!output.timedOut&&!output.overflow&&!output.spawnFailure&&!output.childError&&parsed&&parsed.is_error!==true&&typeof parsed.result==='string'&&parsed.result.trim())return'NORMAL_RESPONSE';
  const text=parsed?.is_error===true?String(parsed.result??'')+' '+JSON.stringify(parsed.errors??[])+' '+output.stderr:output.stderr;
  if(/(?:hit|usage|rate|subscription|session).{0,100}limit|quota|limit.{0,80}reset/is.test(text))return'SUBSCRIPTION_OR_USAGE_LIMIT';
  if(/prompt.{0,50}(long|large)|context.{0,50}(limit|exceed)|too many tokens/is.test(text))return'INPUT_CONTEXT_LIMIT';
  if(/(?:unknown|invalid|unsupported).{0,100}(?:option|argument|model)|model.{0,100}(?:not exist|not available|not have access)/is.test(text))return'UNSUPPORTED_CLI_OR_MODEL';
  if(/authentication|log.?in|not authenticated|unauthorized|expired.{0,50}token|OAuth/is.test(text))return'SUBSCRIPTION_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED';return'CLI_FAILURE_NO_VERDICT';
}

// Parent/provenance correlation owner. No original hashes escape on failure.
export function validateArtifactSenderBinding(valid,capture,outcomeStrict){
 const a=valid.artifact;requireFact(JSON.stringify(a.strictParseEvidence)===JSON.stringify(outcomeStrict));
 requireFact(a.exitCode===capture.exitCode&&a.closeObserved===capture.closeObserved);
 const reasons=[];if(capture.timedOut)reasons.push('TIMEOUT');if(capture.childError)reasons.push('CHILD_ERROR');if(!capture.closeObserved)reasons.push('CLOSE_NOT_OBSERVED');if(capture.overflow)reasons.push('OVERFLOW_TERMINATION');if(capture.signal!==null)reasons.push('SIGNAL_TERMINATION');
 for(const name of ['stdout','stderr']){const stream=a[name],original=capture[name+'Buffer'],truncated=capture[name+'Truncated'];
  requireFact(stream.originalRetainedBytes===original.length&&stream.observedBytes===capture[name+'ObservedBytes']&&stream.truncated===truncated);
  const expected=[...(truncated?['CAP_TRUNCATION']:[]),...reasons];requireFact(JSON.stringify(stream.incompleteReasons)===JSON.stringify(expected));
  if(expected.length)requireFact(stream.mode===(truncated?'TRUNCATED_PREFIX_WITHHELD':'INCOMPLETE_STREAM_WITHHELD'));
  else{requireFact(!stream.mode.endsWith('_WITHHELD'));if(stream.redactedSpans.length===0)requireFact(stream.originalSha256===hash(original));}
 }
 const withheld=capture.stdoutTruncated||reasons.length>0;
 requireFact(withheld?a.strictParseEvidence.status==='NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD':a.strictParseEvidence.status!=='NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD');return valid;
}

// Closed exception carrier; sender allowance remains unchanged.
export function normalizeProcedureException(error){let code;try{code=typeof error?.code==='string'?error.code:'UNSPECIFIED_ERROR';}catch{return'UNRECOGNIZED_CODE';}return[...safeCodes,...procedureCodes].includes(code)?code:'UNRECOGNIZED_CODE';}

// Any finite hit (including screen counter saturation) has one screen refusal code.
function screenSensitive(bytes,budget,escapeAware){try{requireFact(sensitiveSpans(bytes,budget,escapeAware).length===0,'PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED');}catch(e){if(e.code==='RESOURCE_PATTERN_HITS')throw new Refusal('PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED');throw e;}}
export function assertPublicCopyScreen(bytes){screenSensitive(bytes,createBudget(),true);}
export function initialTransformEvidence(workerPin){return{workerPin:{...workerPin},policyId:resourcePolicy.policyId,workerConstructionAttempted:false,workerCreated:false,workerOnlineObserved:false,workerSourceVerified:false,resultMessageObserved:false,exitObserved:false,deadlineExpired:false,terminationRequested:false,exitCode:null,errorCode:'TRANSFORM_STARTUP',parentErrorCode:null,workerOutcomeCode:null,observedElapsedMs:0,deadlineMs:resourcePolicy.workerDeadlineMs,terminationGraceMs:resourcePolicy.terminationGraceMs};}
export function validateTransformResult(result,workerPin,parserPin){
 exactObject(result,['kind','transformEvidence','outcome']);requireFact(result.kind==='RESPONSE_TRANSFORM_RESULT');const e=validateTransformEvidence(result.transformEvidence,workerPin);
 if(e.observationState==='UNKNOWN_AFTER_CALLBACK_FAILURE'){requireFact(result.outcome===null);return result;}
 requireFact(e.resultMessageObserved===(result.outcome!==null),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');
 if(result.outcome!==null){const o=validateWorkerOutcome(result.outcome,parserPin);requireFact(e.workerOutcomeCode===(o.type==='REFUSAL'?o.errorCode:null),'CONTENT_MISMATCH');if(o.type==='REFUSAL'&&o.errorCode==='TRANSFORM_STARTUP')requireFact(!e.workerSourceVerified,'CONTENT_MISMATCH');}
 if(e.errorCode===null)requireFact(result.outcome?.type==='SUCCESS','CONTENT_MISMATCH');return result;
}

===== FULL BODY work/gss-CR03-resource-transform62_20261014.mjs; ORIGINAL BUFFER SHA256 20506A6530D109620B0319FCD879C7E1D7241AF2D6642C198B5196F87815D936; BYTES 5896 =====
// Unadopted FICTIONAL bench transform mechanism. All policy comes from one module.
import * as p from './gss-CR03-resource-policy62_20261014.mjs';
export function transformResponse(capture,parserPin){
  const budget=p.createBudget();let sequence=0,strict=null;
  const next=kind=>'<REDACTED'+(kind==='KEY'?'_KEY':'')+':'+(sequence++)+'>';
  const reasons=[];if(capture.timedOut)reasons.push('TIMEOUT');if(capture.childError)reasons.push('CHILD_ERROR');if(!capture.closeObserved)reasons.push('CLOSE_NOT_OBSERVED');if(capture.overflow)reasons.push('OVERFLOW_TERMINATION');if(capture.signal!==null)reasons.push('SIGNAL_TERMINATION');
  function replace(bytes,spans,markers,limit){let size=bytes.length;for(let i=0;i<spans.length;i++)size+=Buffer.byteLength(markers[i])-(spans[i].endByte-spans[i].startByte);p.requireFact(size<=limit,'RESOURCE_PUBLIC_BYTES');
    const out=Buffer.allocUnsafe(size);let at=0,pos=0;for(let i=0;i<spans.length;i++){const s=spans[i];pos+=bytes.copy(out,pos,at,s.startByte);pos+=out.write(markers[i],pos);at=s.endByte;}bytes.copy(out,pos,at);return out;}
  function ordinalPath(chain){let path='$';for(const suffix of chain)path=p.childPath(path,suffix);return path;}
  function byteRedact(bytes){const spans=p.sensitiveSpans(bytes,budget,true),rows=[];for(const span of spans){const row={...span,marker:next('VALUE')};p.chargeRow(budget,row);rows.push(row);}return{bytes:replace(bytes,spans,rows.map(r=>r.marker),bytes===capture.stdoutBuffer?p.resourcePolicy.publicStdoutBytes:p.resourcePolicy.publicStderrBytes),rows,mode:'BYTE_FALLBACK',coordinate:'ORIGINAL_RETAINED_BYTE_OFFSETS'};}
  function jsonRedact(bytes,value){const rows=[];
    function visit(v,chain){if(typeof v==='string'){const b=Buffer.from(v),spans=p.sensitiveSpans(b,budget,false);if(!spans.length)return v;const path=ordinalPath(chain),local=[];
      for(const s of spans){const row={...s,marker:next('VALUE'),entryPath:path,kind:'VALUE',coordinate:'DECODED_STRING_UTF8_BYTE_OFFSETS'};p.chargeRow(budget,row);rows.push(row);local.push(row);}return replace(b,spans,local.map(r=>r.marker),p.resourcePolicy.publicStdoutBytes).toString('utf8');}
      if(Array.isArray(v))return v.map((x,i)=>visit(x,[...chain,'[array:'+i+']']));
      if(v&&typeof v==='object'){const keys=Object.keys(v),reserved=new Set(keys),out=Object.create(null);for(const [i,k] of keys.entries()){const child=[...chain,'[object-entry:'+i+']'],spans=p.sensitiveSpans(Buffer.from(k),budget,false);let key=k;
        if(spans.length){do{key=next('KEY');}while(reserved.has(key)||Object.hasOwn(out,key));const path=ordinalPath(child);for(const s of spans){const row={...s,marker:key,entryPath:path,kind:'KEY',coordinate:'DECODED_STRING_UTF8_BYTE_OFFSETS'};p.chargeRow(budget,row);rows.push(row);}}
        out[key]=visit(v[k],[...child,'.value']);}return out;}return v;}
    const v=visit(value,[]);return{bytes:rows.length?p.serializePublicStdout(v):Buffer.from(bytes),rows,mode:'JSON_ENVELOPE',coordinate:'DECODED_STRING_UTF8_BYTE_OFFSETS'};
  }
  function make(bytes,observed,truncated,isStdout){
    if(truncated||reasons.length){if(isStdout)strict=p.strictEvidence('NOT_ATTEMPTED_WITHHELD',parserPin);return{base64:'',originalSha256:null,publicSha256:p.hash(Buffer.alloc(0)),originalRetainedBytes:bytes.length,publicRetainedBytes:0,observedBytes:observed,truncated,redactedSpans:[],mode:truncated?'TRUNCATED_PREFIX_WITHHELD':'INCOMPLETE_STREAM_WITHHELD',coordinate:null,incompleteReasons:[...(truncated?['CAP_TRUNCATION']:[]),...reasons],normalizationApplied:false,originalHashWithheldReason:'Incomplete stream may contain a partial secret below finite pattern thresholds'};}
    let r;if(isStdout){let parsed;try{parsed=p.parseRawStdoutStrictJson(bytes);strict=p.strictEvidence('PASS',parserPin);}catch(e){if(e.code==='JSON_DUPLICATE'){strict=p.strictEvidence('DUPLICATE_REFUSED',parserPin);throw new p.Refusal('RAW_STDOUT_DUPLICATE');}if(e.code==='RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT')throw e;strict=p.strictEvidence('MALFORMED_OR_INCOMPLETE',parserPin);}
      if(strict.status==='PASS')r=jsonRedact(bytes,parsed);}
    r??=byteRedact(bytes);p.requireFact(r.bytes.length<=(isStdout?p.resourcePolicy.publicStdoutBytes:p.resourcePolicy.publicStderrBytes),'RESOURCE_PUBLIC_BYTES');
    return{base64:r.bytes.toString('base64'),originalSha256:r.rows.length?null:p.hash(bytes),publicSha256:p.hash(r.bytes),originalRetainedBytes:bytes.length,publicRetainedBytes:r.bytes.length,observedBytes:observed,truncated:false,redactedSpans:r.rows,mode:r.mode,coordinate:r.coordinate,jsonTransformError:null,incompleteReasons:[],normalizationApplied:r.mode==='JSON_ENVELOPE'&&r.rows.length>0,originalHashWithheldReason:r.rows.length?'Original/span hash could confirm a low-entropy redacted value':null};
  }
  try{
    const artifact={scope:'PUBLIC RESPONSE COPY; decoded JSON leaves or labelled byte fallback; incomplete streams withheld',stdout:make(capture.stdoutBuffer,capture.stdoutObservedBytes,capture.stdoutTruncated,true),stderr:make(capture.stderrBuffer,capture.stderrObservedBytes,capture.stderrTruncated,false),exitCode:capture.exitCode,closeObserved:capture.closeObserved,screenLimit:p.responseCopyPolicy,streamCompletenessLimit:'Timeout/child error/missing close/overflow/signal imply withheld streams; stdin error or kill count alone does not, so a closed early-EOF result remains inspectable. Not a receiver or process-tree proof.',receiverBytesProved:false,automaticRetry:false,resourcePolicy:p.resourcePolicy,strictParseEvidence:strict};
    const copy=p.serializePublicCopy(artifact);p.assertPublicCopyScreen(copy);
    return{type:'SUCCESS',copy,sha256:p.hash(copy),bytes:copy.length,strictParseEvidence:strict};
  }catch(e){if(!p.workerRefusalCodes.includes(e?.code)||e.code==='TRANSFORM_STARTUP')throw new p.Refusal('UNRECOGNIZED_CODE');return{type:'REFUSAL',errorCode:e.code,strictParseEvidence:strict,publicResponseHashes:null};}
}

// FICTIONAL bench only; caller-derived entry pin, literal worker dependencies.
// Deadline uses an exact observed event timestamp; evidence is a frozen snapshot.
import fs from 'node:fs';import path from 'node:path';import {pathToFileURL} from 'node:url';import {performance} from 'node:perf_hooks';import {Worker} from 'node:worker_threads';import * as p from './gss-CR03-resource-policy62_20261014.mjs';
const ROOT='C:\\Users\\14bez\\Downloads\\Booster Shop\\booster-shop-ops';
function freeze(value){if(value&&typeof value==='object'){for(const v of Object.values(value))freeze(v);Object.freeze(value);}return value;}
export async function runFictionalWorker(dto,workerPin,parserPin,dependencyPins){
  const start=performance.now(),e=p.initialTransformEvidence(workerPin);let worker=null,outcome=null,deadlineTimer=null,graceTimer=null;
  return await new Promise(resolve=>{let settled=false;
    const elapsed=now=>now-start;
    function observeDeadline(now){if(elapsed(now)>=e.deadlineMs)e.deadlineExpired=true;}
    function requestTermination(){if(!worker)return;e.terminationRequested=true;try{Promise.resolve(worker.terminate()).catch(()=>{});}catch{}}
    function finish(now){if(settled)return;settled=true;clearTimeout(deadlineTimer);clearTimeout(graceTimer);
      e.observedElapsedMs=Math.ceil(elapsed(now));e.errorCode=p.deriveTransformCode(e);if(e.errorCode==='TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL'&&e.parentErrorCode===null)e.parentErrorCode='PROTOCOL_REFUSED';
      if(e.workerCreated&&!e.exitObserved){requestTermination();worker.unref();}
      const snapshot=freeze(structuredClone(e));const observedOutcome=outcome===null?null:structuredClone(outcome);
      resolve({kind:'RESPONSE_TRANSFORM_RESULT',transformEvidence:snapshot,outcome:observedOutcome});}
    function stop(now){if(settled)return;if(!e.workerCreated){finish(now);return;}if(!e.terminationRequested){requestTermination();graceTimer=setTimeout(()=>finish(performance.now()),e.terminationGraceMs);}}
    function protocol(now){if(settled)return;if(e.parentErrorCode!=='ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY')e.parentErrorCode='PROTOCOL_REFUSED';stop(now);}
    // Timer begins at the same monotonic start, before synchronous re-admission.
    deadlineTimer=setTimeout(()=>{if(settled)return;e.deadlineExpired=true;stop(performance.now());},e.deadlineMs);
    try{
      p.requireFact(process.cwd()===ROOT&&process.version==='v24.17.0'&&process.execArgv.length===0&&!Object.hasOwn(process.env,'NODE_OPTIONS'),'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');p.requireFact(workerPin.path==='work/gss-CR03-resource-worker62_20261014.mjs','TRANSFORM_STARTUP');p.validateWorkerRequest(dto,workerPin);
      const physical=path.join(ROOT,workerPin.path),b=fs.readFileSync(physical),s=fs.lstatSync(physical);p.requireFact(s.isFile()&&!s.isSymbolicLink()&&s.nlink===1&&fs.realpathSync.native(physical)===physical&&p.hash(b)===workerPin.sha256&&b.length===workerPin.bytes,'TRANSFORM_STARTUP');
      const beforeConstructor=performance.now();observeDeadline(beforeConstructor);if(e.deadlineExpired){finish(beforeConstructor);return;}
      e.workerConstructionAttempted=true;worker=new Worker(pathToFileURL(physical),{workerData:{fictionalBench:true,expectedEntryPin:{...workerPin},dependencyPins:structuredClone(dependencyPins),request:dto},transferList:[dto.stdout,dto.stderr],env:{},execArgv:[],stdout:true,stderr:true,resourceLimits:{maxOldGenerationSizeMb:p.resourcePolicy.workerOldGenerationMiB,stackSizeMb:p.resourcePolicy.workerStackMiB}});e.workerCreated=true;
      worker.on('online',()=>{if(settled)return;const now=performance.now();observeDeadline(now);e.workerOnlineObserved=true;if(e.deadlineExpired)stop(now);});
      const output=()=>protocol(performance.now());worker.stdout.on('data',output);worker.stderr.on('data',output);worker.on('messageerror',output);
      worker.on('message',m=>{if(settled)return;const now=performance.now();observeDeadline(now);try{
        if(m?.type==='SOURCE_VERIFIED'){p.exactObject(m,['type','entryPin','dependencyPins'],'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');p.requireFact(!e.workerSourceVerified&&!e.resultMessageObserved&&JSON.stringify(m.entryPin)===JSON.stringify(workerPin)&&JSON.stringify(m.dependencyPins)===JSON.stringify(dependencyPins),'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');e.workerSourceVerified=true;}
        else{p.requireFact(!e.resultMessageObserved,'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');p.validateWorkerOutcome(m,parserPin);p.requireFact(e.workerSourceVerified||m.type==='REFUSAL'&&m.errorCode==='TRANSFORM_STARTUP','TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');if(m.type==='REFUSAL'&&m.errorCode==='TRANSFORM_STARTUP')p.requireFact(!e.workerSourceVerified,'TRANSFORM_PROTOCOL');outcome=structuredClone(m);e.resultMessageObserved=true;e.workerOutcomeCode=m.type==='REFUSAL'?m.errorCode:null;}
        if(e.deadlineExpired)stop(now);
      }catch{protocol(now);}});
      worker.on('error',err=>{if(settled)return;const now=performance.now();observeDeadline(now);e.parentErrorCode=err?.code==='ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY'?'ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY':e.parentErrorCode==='PROTOCOL_REFUSED'?'PROTOCOL_REFUSED':'WORKER_ERROR';stop(now);});
      worker.on('exit',code=>{if(settled)return;const now=performance.now();observeDeadline(now);e.exitObserved=true;e.exitCode=code;finish(now);});
    }catch{const now=performance.now();observeDeadline(now);if(e.workerCreated){e.parentErrorCode='WORKER_ERROR';stop(now);}else finish(now);}
  });

# GSS working agreement

Date: 2026-09-28
Authority: explicit owner instructions in the GSS planning conversation.
Applies to: GSS series only, currently planned inside the Booster Shop ops repository.

## 1. Priority and scope

This is the GSS-specific working contract referenced by root `AGENTS.md`.
Current explicit owner instructions override it. Within GSS, it overrides
conflicting older Booster role, authorship, Git-authority and sequence rules.
Outside GSS, existing Booster rules remain unchanged. GSS work touching a
shared production integration still needs its existing deployment/data-write
authority; an identifier prefix does not waive that boundary.

The consolidated functional baseline revision 1 was approved under D059 on
2026-09-30; see `../plans/GSS_scope-baseline_20260930.md`. Current work advances
to architecture and decomposition. The earlier 18-package draft is an inventory
of candidates, not an approved implementation backlog. Functional approval does
not settle explicit open rules or authorize code/deployment before task readiness.

## 2. Equal agents, one assigned executor

Codex and Claude have equal authority to architect, implement, propose
solutions, review each other and commit/push their assigned GSS work. Claude
may use Claude Code or another available execution surface. Practical tool
limitations do not transfer ownership automatically.

The owner assigns each task. One assigned executor owns its implementation,
task-local evidence and scope accounting. Review does not authorize the
reviewer to overwrite the executor's files concurrently. A handoff/reassignment
must make writer ownership explicit.

D064 (2026-10-03) permits either executor to request peer review directly from
the other, discuss findings and iterate fixes within the approved assigned task.
This authorizes task-specific review communication where tools permit it; it
does not transfer implementation ownership or authorize unrelated messages.

Commit/push permission is a standing owner grant for approved, assigned GSS
scope, not a fresh permission question at every commit. Inspect the diff and
staged set, include only task-owned files/hunks, preserve unrelated changes,
and report what was committed/pushed. Do not force-push or rewrite shared
history under this routine grant. Follow the repository's autosync sentinel
procedure when performing Git writes. Git permission is not deployment or
live business-data write permission.

Both agents may challenge a decision and propose an alternative. Neither may
silently substitute an outcome the owner has approved.

## 3. Owner decisions and changes

Record an owner proposal/approval that changes the initial task direction as
an owner decision. A decision entry contains:

- stable decision ID and date;
- owner instruction or a faithful summary;
- accepted outcome and scope;
- affected blocks/tasks, processes, interfaces, data and acceptance criteria;
- what earlier decision/proposal it supersedes;
- evidence/reference and unresolved follow-ups.

D049 confirms that an authorized agent may record an actual owner approval
with its source/provenance. The owner remains the approver of functional
changes and acceptance. Executor evidence and independent reviewer findings
are separate attestations; an agent cannot approve on the owner's behalf.

D066 requires chat-derived decisions recorded in GSS to remain pending until
the authenticated owner confirms the exact target there. Provide Confirm/Reject
and explicit batch confirmation; agents cannot verify their own pasted evidence.
This governs future GSS records/imports, not the validity of direct owner
instructions during pre-GSS planning. D067 selects an in-app attention counter
initially, with Telegram notifications as the next extension.

Preserve history: an amended decision supersedes an earlier one rather than
erasing why the implementation changed. Distinguish accepted owner decisions
from executor recommendations and unresolved questions.

Changes to final functionality require the owner to be informed and approve
the change before dependent implementation. Routine technical choices inside
the approved behavior, constraints and authority are the executor's work.
When a review exposes a missing functional decision, present the consequence
and a recommended choice; continue unaffected work.

## 4. Autonomy and owner involvement

Executors carry authorized work through source inspection, design, local
implementation, tests, review fixes, documentation and scoped Git operations.
Do not delegate tool-accessible diagnostics or routine commands to the owner
merely for convenience.

Involve the owner for business/product decisions, final interface QA, disputed
tradeoffs affecting outcomes, and operations impossible or disproportionately
costly with available tools. Present a concrete, reviewable proposal and its
effect rather than an open-ended permission request. Existing production
gates remain until expressly changed.

Owner QA is not a substitute for executor testing. Label source inspection,
local tests, integration tests, deployment, live proof and owner acceptance
separately.

## 5. Definition before implementation

D064 confirms that approval of a sufficiently designed block plus assignment
of its executor covers autonomous preparation/execution of its scoped subtasks;
do not request permission for every routine step. Independent research, technical
choices, implementation, testing/fixes, scoped refactoring, documentation and
task updates are included. Owner gates for functional changes, assignment,
cross-block priorities, paid activation/spending increases, production/live-data
changes and applicable owner QA remain. Pause only the dependent work when a
decision is needed. D065 approves block-level autonomy, bounded subagents,
automatic progression through ready assigned work, routine technical subtasks,
review/fix loops, local checks and consolidated reporting. The accepted detailed
policy is `../plans/GSS_autonomy-options_20261003.md`. Cross-block progression
requires an explicitly owner-approved queue; assignment and owner gates persist.

The queue is an immutable ordered version with an executor and permitted phase
per entry and a matching owner-confirmation reference. Modification requires a
new confirmation; readiness never supplies missing queue authority. Current
queue authority is recorded in the working map: D069 approves Q1 revision 2, foundation -> roadmap -> adoption -> Telegram, with Codex execution and Claude review. Adoption includes D048 reconciliation, trial import, restore drill and owner-gated canonical cutover.

The executor proposes optimal continuous-execution segments within each block:
scope, deliverable, dependencies, verification/review, delegation if useful,
natural boundary and stop conditions. The owner should not have to guess these
boundaries. Record them in the working roadmap and explain them in the nub opening.
This adds planning visibility, not repeated approval for routine subtasks.
Subagents inherit scoped authority; avoid competing file/Git/status writers and
retain one accountable primary executor. A subagent review does not replace
required other-executor review or owner QA.

The owner requires two rounds on retained and new functionality: one with
Codex and one with Claude. Both use the current audited system and the same
owner decisions. Record agreements, alternatives, missing processes and
contradictions in one comparison register. The owner settles the functional
boundary before technical implementation starts.

Owner-approved sequence (2026-09-28, GSS-D014; future amendments remain possible):

1. Codex + owner review the capability catalogue: keep, redesign, add, defer,
   retire; state actor, outcome, inputs/outputs and related processes.
2. Claude reviews and extends that catalogue, explicitly identifying omissions
   and alternative solutions rather than treating the first round as settled.
3. Consolidate both rounds and obtain the owner's overall functional boundary.
4. Work out the system architecture and cross-process contracts; decompose the
   plan into blocks -> tasks -> subtasks with acceptance evidence.
5. Prepare each block and each task deeply before its implementation. Resolve
   material cross-block dependencies early; keep speculative detail out of
   implementation until the relevant decisions are settled.
6. Build the required foundation, then deliver the roadmap as the first working
   feature. Manage later accepted work through that roadmap after its own
   source-of-truth transition is defined and accepted.

GSS-D015 confirms the design cadence: agree the overall system logic and
cross-process relationships in advance, then prepare detailed technical
specifications sequentially before each block. This is not permission to code
an under-specified task or to postpone a dependency decision needed now.

D064/D065 authorize routine scoped other-executor review requests and bounded
subagents without new per-request owner permission. Tool availability must be
verified; task ownership and owner decisions are not transferred by dispatch.
Unrelated collaboration or reassignment remains outside that grant.

GSS-D033 clarifies delivery: provide owner-visible increments for testing and
gradual adoption, not a single unseen all-at-once operational release. Earlier
first-launch language denotes prioritized scope, not an obligation to finish
all those capabilities before owner QA. Keep the foundation/roadmap-first
sequence and explicitly distinguish preview/test readiness from live adoption.
For each live transition, specify the authoritative system and active writers
for the affected data/process; partial delivery must not create two competing
stock, order or financial masters. Production deployment/write gates remain.
Integration priorities follow D030–D032: Nova Poshta first among carriers,
Checkbox strongly desired, Ukrposhta/Meest potentially deferred indefinitely,
and banking low/very low depending on complexity. No fixed dates are implied.

If an unresolved rule affects foundation design, surface that dependency
earlier rather than silently choosing a default. Reservation before payment
and hold/expiry behavior remain proposals until this clarification occurs.

GSS-D045 confirms two stages: settle architecture-shaping D024 questions before
ledger-module design, then detailed rules before each affected block. The
roadmap foundation is not blocked by unrelated financial details.

Every block has a short owner-facing opening summary using the `nub` skill:

> Scope: what this block includes and where its responsibility ends.
> Result: what the owner/staff will be able to do when it is accepted.
> Architecture: in plain Ukrainian, which parts exchange/store information.
> Model: recommended model and reasoning/thinking depth, with a brief reason.

Use the same summary for an individual task when its complexity, risk or new
decision warrants it. Durable specifications may be English; owner summaries
are concise Ukrainian.

Before implementation, the block/task specification must address the relevant:

- user workflows and permission boundaries;
- entities, invariants, state transitions, input/output/API contracts;
- dependencies in both directions and effects on other business processes;
- normal, exceptional, cancelled, repeated, partially failed and reversed cases;
- financial, stock, date/timezone and identity consequences;
- integration failure/retry/concurrency behavior;
- migration, compatibility, observability and rollback;
- concrete acceptance criteria and appropriate automated/manual QA.

Apply proportional depth: a small task can inherit an established block
contract and identify its exact delta. Do not skip analysis of affected
processes, but do not repeatedly reopen unrelated settled systems.


===== END BODY =====

  const record={actualStartUtc,actualEndUtc,recorderSha256,cliBinarySha256,date:new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10),partition:packet.partition,scope:packet.title,reviewer:'Claude Code',cliVersion:'2.1.299',transport:'installed subscription CLI; tools/MCP/hooks disabled; no session persistence; no paid-provider fallback',requestedModel:MODEL,effort:'high',resolvedModels:models,exitCode:output.exitCode,closeObserved:output.closed,isError:parsed.is_error??false,durationMs:parsed.duration_ms,wallMs:output.wallMs,manifestSha256:manifestHash,packetSha256:packet.sha256,packetBytes:packet.bytes,reviewUsable:usable,inputsUnchangedAfter:unchangedAfter,verdict:headers[0]??null,usageByModel:usage,estimatedCostUsd:parsed.total_cost_usd,costInterpretation:'CLI estimate, not a paid API charge or measured subscription quota debit',nativeAuthority:false,retryAuthorized:false,applicationAuthorized:false,phase2Authorized:false,productApproval:false,wholeFoundationAccepted:false,result:parsed.result};
  write(runPath,record);
  write(mdPath,`# GSS actual Foundation CR03 STATIC intake/design review — partition ${packet.partition}\n\nDate:${actualEndUtc.slice(0,10)}. Reviewer:Claude Code2.1.299; model(s):${models.join(', ')}; effort=high.\nExact packet SHA256:${packet.sha256}; ${packet.bytes} UTF-8 bytes.\nManifest SHA256:${manifestHash}.\nTools/MCP/hooks disabled; Foundation CR03 static intake/map/design review only; no native replay, target/private restore, pilot completion or owner gate grant.\nCapture: complete actual model result below, without paraphrase.\nUsable:${usable}; exact inputs unchanged after:${unchangedAfter}.\n\n---\n\n${parsed.result}\n`);
  process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({event:'Claude review captured',partition:packet.partition,usable,verdict:record.verdict,durationMs:record.durationMs,models})+'\n');
  if(!usable){process.exitCode=1;break;}
}

===== FULL BODY plans/GSS_CR03_escaped-byte-screen_owner-choice_PROPOSED_20261014.md; ORIGINAL BUFFER SHA256 1C75804C3DF3ABAEA9BC1384CC0FFC19E9EC206114DC597A0822AECC0EB3F886; BYTES 4106 =====
# Escaped byte-copy behavior — concrete owner choice, PROPOSED

Date:2026-10-14. Actual61 DESIGN Changes requested B1 requires correcting and resolving a new functional loss consequence before implementation. GSS-D005 reserves changed accepted functional outcomes for informed owner approval. Final14 numeric approval acknowledged shared-hit/metadata exhaustion (including stderr) and journal-save loss only. Neither approval nor generic continuation acknowledged the new decoded-byte refusal below.

Both options DROP the rejected100000 byte-token quota. maxEnvelopeNodes stays raw values+keys only. Do not pair quotes: use a context-free forward escape-decoded finite view, handling recognized simple escapes/odd-backslash Unicode escapes and surrogate pairs regardless of quote parity. Unknown/malformed escapes remain raw and disclosed as finite assurance limits. A bounded view per public stream has length<=the already approved byte cap, can be scanned as one buffer with existing linear finite cursors, then released; no per-token regex calls or extra token-count refusal. No number or parent hard deadline is added. Whole-requester memory and parent work remain outside256MiB worker heap/15s worker budget.

Option A (recommended for implementation simplicity): keep existing BYTE redaction semantics and add the stronger decoded finite screen. A newly decoded finite sensitive pattern in either public byte stream refuses the WHOLE copy with PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED, zero C/result and no automatic repeat. In particular, valid JSON_ENVELOPE stdout with a usable verdict can be lost because stderr contains a pattern written with Unicode escapes, even when shared budgets are nowhere near exhausted. This is a THIRD response-loss path. For malformed byte stdout the same refusal can replace a useful auth/quota/context/unsupported-model diagnostic with a generic BUILD refusal. Disclose both effects explicitly. Retain14 numbers and existing worker9 refusal codes. Worker and parent/provenance share the same public-only finite predicate; private-original fidelity remains runtime-only. Independent DESIGN and CODE review must accept exact tables/test/corpus before real use.

Option B: design stronger byte redaction instead of unconditional decoded-hit refusal. Decode for detection, map the detected ranges back to ORIGINAL retained byte offsets and replace those raw encoded ranges with markers. This requires a separately reviewed linear source-coordinate mapping algorithm and differential evidence; no second policy, hidden callback or original/span hashes. It changes BYTE redaction semantics and can increase hit/metadata usage and trigger the ALREADY acknowledged shared-limit refusal. Diagnostic categories can change if their distinguishing words lie inside newly redacted spans; it does not guarantee preservation of every diagnosis. Parent fidelity and stronger public screen still apply. This is more code, memory/performance work and acceptance cases; no evidence yet that it is faster or safer to implement.

Illustrative generated-fictional effect, no literal address: stdout contains a valid verdict; stderr contains a quoted address whose separator is written as backslash-u0040. Existing62 leaves that encoded token. Option A refuses the entire artifact; option B masks the encoded raw range and keeps the rest only if all original and public limits/predicates pass. Tests must cover escaped keys/JWT/URL forms, stray quotes/newlines/parity, both streams, valid-normal stdout plus encoded stderr, diagnostics, and every newly-refused or deliberately transformed62-accepted fixture.

Selecting either option authorizes its scoped local design and later independently reviewed implementation under existing governance. It does NOT activate real responses, accept SOURCE/STD, waive F1–F6/B2/B3, permit a provider/native replay, paid API, private/live/production or Git/status changes. Raw owner reply must bind this exact proposal and actual61 context; do not fabricate authenticated GSS confirmation. Continue unaffected B2/B3/N2–N8 design while this choice is pending.

===== END BODY =====

# GSS actual Foundation CR03 STATIC intake/design review — partition INTAKE61

Date:2026-10-14. Reviewer:Claude Code2.1.299; model(s):claude-opus-5-5; effort=high.
Exact packet SHA256:DAA23659775E9458AB4B84C0F78E0623322AB8CE2C1F2278ED3616C2B8021EF5; 642567 UTF-8 bytes.
Manifest SHA256:95756205F2CD9D90A1D62755212F9FEF28D917976CCFBA201C2FF29469A61C0D.
Tools/MCP/hooks disabled; Foundation CR03 static intake/map/design review only; no native replay, target/private restore, pilot completion or owner gate grant.
Capture: complete actual model result below, without paraphrase.
Usable:true; exact inputs unchanged after:true.

---

## Verdict: Changes requested
## Scope: GSS FOUNDATION INTAKE61

**Subject.** Proposed DESIGN63b `216EDD2B…` (27389 bytes).

**Assessed against:**
- contract58 `1F4F0879…`;
- the accepted62 corrective boundary, the r60 basis and the INTAKE60 disposition;
- the complete actual60 result, its terminal and the supplemental identity record;
- whole policy62, transform62, supervisor62, bench62 and worker62, plus their publishers, callers and reports;
- helper24 and the Design43 EXCERPTS;
- the final owner numeric record and its proposal;
- the review61 checker, builder and verifier chain.

**Method.** I read the embedded bodies only. I executed nothing and recomputed no hashes. U+0040 is written `\u0040`.

**Context.** The context is sufficient for a DESIGN verdict:
- The bodies that own the changed behaviour are whole: policy62, transform62, supervisor62, bench62, worker62, contract58 and the actual60 feedback.
- The superseded 63 draft and the 59/51 evidence are identity-only, and no conclusion below depends on them.
- The six dependencies omitted from review60 are now identity-pinned.
- I traced every file read by `verifyPrimary` across the review59, review60 and review61 checkers, and by the builder and verifier.
  - Each path I could resolve maps to a full body or an identity record.
  - Paths named only inside identity-only receipts (encoding, excerpt and producer pins) are taken from those receipts, not inspected.
- The 14 numeric controls in policy62 equal the owner record exactly, and `gss-CR03-review61-design-checks` asserts this mechanically.

### Overall assessment

The core of F2 is the right remedy for actual60 F2/N4:
- the parent re-derives the expected copy from the private original;
- the parent's raw strict decision is taken first, so a forged worker root cannot survive;
- zero-span stdout must equal the exact original bytes;
- there is no self-attested fidelity flag.

The other F-items are sound in substance:
- **F3:** sample the clock in the deadline callback and re-arm if early.
- **F4:** keep a single clone and use an O(depth) traversal.
- **F5:** refuse AFTER_JSON_ERROR in both streams and normalize codes.
- **F6:** pin dependencies and assert terminal scalars.

Three defects block acceptance:
1. F1 misstates its own loss effects and creates undisclosed stderr-driven loss.
2. The parent refusal union is not reconciled with the closed tables in contract58.
3. The acceptance matrix cannot detect common-mode errors in the relocated redactor.

### Blocking findings

**B1. F1 creates new stderr loss paths, and the disclosed consequence is false.**

The design says the tightened predicate "can refuse malformed copies that previously survived… Such copies never produced usable strict-parser results." That holds for stdout BYTE_FALLBACK. It does not hold for stderr:
- **Stderr is always byte-mode.** In transform62 `make()`, a complete stderr always goes through `byteRedact`.
- **F1 covers stderr explicitly.** It applies to "BOTH BYTE_FALLBACK streams", with a token count "across the two byte-mode public streams".

As a result, a NORMAL_RESPONSE with a valid JSON_ENVELOPE verdict on stdout is now refused at BUILD with PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED in either of these cases:
- **(a) Too many quote tokens.** Stderr contains more than 100000 quote-delimited candidates, which is about 200001 quote bytes within 1 MiB. A verbose JSON log dump would do it.
- **(b) An escaped finite pattern.** Stderr contains a lexically valid token whose decoded form hits a finite pattern, for example `x\u0040domain.tld`. Worker byte redaction with `escapeAware:true` never decodes `\u` escapes, so the token survives into public stderr. The new parent screen then refuses the whole copy.

The owner record acknowledges only two loss paths: shared hit/metadata exhaustion (including by stderr) and journal persistence failure. It does not acknowledge these.

Two further disclosure problems:
- **`maxEnvelopeNodes` changes meaning.** It was presented to the owner as "raw values plus keys". Reusing it as a byte-token count that includes stderr is a new control with an old number, not "not a new number".
- **Diagnostic loss on stdout.** For byte-mode stdout, a refusal now turns a CLASSIFICATION_FAILURE (quota, auth, context or unsupported-model diagnosis) into a generic BUILD exception. That diagnostic loss should be disclosed too.

Required fix: correct the consequence text, then either remove the new loss paths or put them before the owner under D005/§3. Recommended remedy:
- **Drop the token quota.** Its only real job is to bound per-call screening overhead on the main thread, outside the watchdog. Achieve that without a refusal by screening decoded content in bounded batches joined with LF:
  - No finite pattern can cross LF. The EMAIL, JWT and AUTH classes exclude it.
  - The `emailMatches` sticky resume treats LF as a boundary.
  - `jwtMatches` resets `first`/`second` on any non-dot, non-JWT byte.
  - Batching is therefore equivalent per token and linear.
- **Decide case (b) explicitly.** Either disclose it as a third stderr loss path with owner acknowledgment, or redact escaped hits. Redaction is a semantic change to the accepted24 byte redaction and would need its own differential evidence.

**B2. The parent refusal union is not closed against contract58's tables.**

- **The code set is not enumerated.** The refusal arm carries "a closed normalized procedure/safe code" but does not list its members.
- **A new duplicate combination has no row.** The combination is a KNOWN worker SUCCESS (`errorCode:null`, `workerOutcomeCode:null`) plus a parent-detected RAW_STDOUT_DUPLICATE with root DUPLICATE_REFUSED.
  - Contract58 states that a duplicate "ALWAYS returns a worker REFUSAL", and worker62/policy62 `validateWorkerOutcome` couples the two.
  - Contract58's explicit parent-failure enumeration has no such row.
  - Under contract58's "every complete no-row combination CONTRADICTORY_OUTPUTS", the combination would therefore close REFUSED for the wrong reason.
  - State the supersession explicitly: phase4 UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA applies whichever side detected it. Define the root, the evidence correlation and the provenance derivation.
- **Retained resource codes are ambiguous.** For a parent-detected original-side RESOURCE_PATTERN_HITS, RESOURCE_METADATA_BYTES, RESOURCE_ENTRY_PATH, RESOURCE_PUBLIC_BYTES or RESOURCE_PARSE_LIMIT after a worker SUCCESS, the design "retains the accepted finite resource code".
  - That code is then indistinguishable from a genuine worker refusal, yet the transformEvidence reports success.
  - Contract58's generic BUILD row is keyed to worker-side failures.
  - Recommendation: any original-side disagreement with a worker SUCCESS maps to PUBLIC_COPY_INVALID, because it is a worker or collector defect. Duplicate is the exception and keeps its phase4 precedence through the root.
- **Required deliverables.** Enumerate the exact rows: failedStep, state, errorCode, root nullability, transformEvidence correlation and closing outcome. Add a test for each.

**B3. The acceptance matrix cannot discriminate common-mode reimplementation errors.**

F2 moves the redactor out of transform62 into CONTEXT, and rewrites it as a planner plus a two-mode streaming renderer. Both the worker collector and the parent comparator use the same planner. The F2 falsifier "compare collector output to comparator" is therefore common-mode. Planner errors would be reproduced on both sides and pass F2:
- marker numbering or gaps;
- KEY reservation;
- row order and pattern-ID order;
- entry ordinals;
- number, lone-surrogate or BOM normalization.

The F2 mutation tests only catch worker-side divergence.

Contract58 already required a differential against accepted24; the 63b matrix drops it. Required matrix row:
- **What to compare.** A byte-for-byte differential of the complete canonical copy, the span rows, the strict root, the AFTER projection and the classification, between the successor and frozen transform62/policy62.
- **Which fixtures.** Every generated in-budget fixture, including:
  - the three 62 extra layouts;
  - the 62 MAX layouts;
  - the new wide/deep layout;
  - collision, integer-key, lone-surrogate, BOM and number-normalization cases.
- **Allowed differences.** Only the enumerated intentional ones: policyId, screenLimit text and fictional63 labels.
- **Loss disclosure.** List every 62-accepted artifact that the new F1 predicate now refuses. That list is the concrete loss disclosure for B1.

### Non-blocking findings (required before CODE unless marked optional)

**N1. The F1 quote-parity weakness defeats the scan's purpose for some inputs.**
- The scanner pairs quotes from the first unescaped quote. "Consume the closing quote even on invalid candidates" fixes the parity for the rest of the buffer.
- A stray odd quote, or an invalid candidate that spans a raw newline in multi-line text, shifts the parity. A later valid escaped token then sits *between* candidates and is never decoded.
- Example shape: `"abc "x\u0040domain.tld"` where the trailing quote opens an unterminated candidate.
- The raw escapeAware screen does not catch it either.
- Recommendation: a context-free escape-decoded view, screened with `escapeAware:false`.
  - Decode `\uXXXX`, surrogate pairs and simple escapes wherever the backslash run is odd.
  - It is linear, independent of parity, subsumes the token-validity rules and composes with the B1 batching.
- Minimum alternative: terminate an invalid candidate at its first raw control byte.
- Add tests for both shapes.

**N2. Provenance composition is not defined.**
- Contract58 has provenance invoke the SAME CONTEXT artifact validator as the parent.
- The new parent entry needs the private capture, which provenance cannot have.
- Define the runtime entry as the shared public-only validator (including the F1 decoded screen) plus the original-fidelity layer.
- Provenance calls exactly the public-only function.
- Test that both paths agree on every public predicate, and that provenance refuses the F1 escaped-token cases.

**N3. Two F3 edge cases need specifying and testing.**
- **Grace finish.** A finish from the grace timer does not call `observeDeadline`. Add it to every finish path, so the proposed `!deadlineExpired ⇒ observedElapsedMs ≤ deadlineMs` correlation becomes a true invariant.
- **Protocol fault near the deadline.**
  - In 62, `stop()` does not clear the deadline timer.
  - Consider a protocol fault at 14990 whose exit arrives after 15000. The deadline timer sets `deadlineExpired`, so TIMEOUT outranks PROTOCOL.
  - This is truthful under the precedence rules, but specify it and add tests for a protocol fault near the deadline and for grace crossing the deadline.
- **Fixture update.** The instrumented fixture's exact `setTimeout` replacement count (2) must change when re-arm is added.

**N4. The F4 receipt clone is overhead for real Workers, and two memory limits need disclosure.**
- **Redundant clone.** A transferred ArrayBuffer is exclusively parent-owned, and non-transferred data was already copied by deserialization. The receipt clone therefore costs up to 24 MiB in production only to protect test fakes. A fixture can post fresh copies instead. (Optional, but justify the choice if the clone is kept.)
- **Deserialization precedes validation.** It happens before any handler runs, so "reject giant nested objects" bounds post-receipt work only.
- **Heap limit coverage.** Worker `resourceLimits` do not bound ArrayBuffer or Buffer backing stores, such as serializer pages and `Buffer.from` results.

**N5. F2 adds parent cost outside both the 15 s budget and the 256 MiB cap.**
- **Reachability.** State explicitly that the recomputation is reached only after a worker SUCCESS.
- **Sequencing.** Order the raw-original graph and the public-stdout leaf-screen graph so they are never live at the same time.
- **Profiling.** The F4 profile must sample the parent BUILD step-2 separately. Report it against the 392622080-byte sample the owner saw.
- **Timing.** Disclose that parent BUILD wall time roughly doubles and has no deadline.

**N6. Renderer equivalence is simpler than described.** (Optional.)
- The transformed bytes are always valid UTF-8, because `Buffer.from` of a JS string plus ASCII markers spliced at ASCII boundaries cannot produce an invalid sequence.
- The only normalization is lone surrogate to U+FFFD in redacted strings, plus carrying partial code points across chunk boundaries.
- Name the exact decoder. `Buffer.toString` keeps U+FEFF; a default TextDecoder strips a leading BOM.

**N7. F6 and transport nits.** (Optional.)
- **Tautological assertion.** `cliVersion` is a literal that the requester writes, so asserting it is tautological. The observed bindings are `cliBinarySha256` (checked at start only) and `resolvedModels`.
- **Stale label.** The r61 requester's "proposed transport19" error label is stale. It must stay, to preserve exact-delta equality with requester60.

**N8. F5 table and union updates.**
- Confirm that contract58's JOURNAL row admits PUBLIC_COPY_SCREEN_REFUSED.
- Add the final 63 parent refusal codes from B2 to the closed `normalizeProcedureException` union.

### Coverage ledger dispositions

| Row | Disposition |
|---|---|
| F1/F2 fidelity and byte-string screen | **Not acceptable yet.** B1, B2 and B3 are blocking; N1, N2 and N6 apply. The F2 architecture itself is sound. The public provenance limit is honestly disclosed. |
| F3/F4 timer, ownership, memory, fixture | **Substantively adequate.** N3, N4 and N5 apply. No tests exist yet for 63. |
| F5 codes, modes and the 14 numbers | **Numbers equal the owner record.** The B1 loss claim is inaccurate. B2 and N8 apply. |
| F6 dependency graph and terminal assertions | **Adequate as identity and framing.** The six dependencies are pinned for this review only, with no retrospective interval proof. N7 is optional. |

### Material limits

- **Nature of this review.** This is a DESIGN reading only. No 63 code, fixture, profile or differential exists. All tests remain obligations.
- **Fidelity is runtime-only.** Private-original fidelity is a runtime check that later public provenance cannot re-establish.
- **Unproved:**
  - privacy beyond the finite policy;
  - whole-process memory, peak usage and OS scheduling;
  - OS pipes, Windows EOF, provider behaviour, native behaviour and forced exit.
- **Still open:**
  - N5 inherited, receiver bytes, hash/import/execution races, clock, forgery, managed-policy hooks and binary enforcement;
  - the frozen17 limits;
  - the full trust registry, the five-pin and three-pin closures, REQUESTER_PRODUCER, physical journals, six-primary provenance, normative30/437 and terminal64;
